**[00:45:17]** God. And of course, he wrote it through

**[00:45:19]** the hands of other people,

**[00:45:21]** but you can tell that there's one

**[00:45:23]** consistent message. So we should be in the process

**[00:45:25]** of

**[00:45:27]** cross-referencing things all the time,

**[00:45:29]** and understanding

**[00:45:31]** cross-references. Like, for example, on my

**[00:45:33]** ESV, my physical Bible,

**[00:45:35]** at the bottom, actually when you're reading,

**[00:45:37]** there's like a little letter

**[00:45:39]** or something, a footnote, and you

**[00:45:41]** go and you see the cross-references. And that's

**[00:45:43]** a great practice to get into.

**[00:45:45]** So you say, oh, I wonder why is this cross-referenced?

**[00:45:47]** And sometimes the cross-references aren't super

**[00:45:51]** So you have to just get in the habit of saying, oh,

**[00:45:53]** what does Isaiah say about, you know,

**[00:45:55]** Corinthians? What does, you know,

**[00:45:57]** Job say about, you know, the Romans?

**[00:46:03]** profound, and it gets you to think in terms of

**[00:46:07]** you really build your knowledge of the Bible. It's not

**[00:46:09]** memorizing verses, although that's,

**[00:46:11]** that can be useful. It's not memorizing

**[00:46:17]** engaging the text, and

**[00:46:19]** engaging and building a relationship

**[00:46:21]** with the text, and building relationships

**[00:46:23]** between other texts.

**[00:46:25]** How is this one like this one?

**[00:46:29]** What are the same themes? What are the same pictures?

**[01:03:07]** that I will reiterate over and over again

**[01:03:09]** is that you want to get in the habit

**[01:03:11]** of building a case for

**[01:03:13]** your beliefs. Too many people

**[01:03:15]** today go for the low-hanging

**[01:03:17]** fruit, which is going to be one of the things

**[01:03:19]** that we'll talk about when we go to

**[01:03:21]** fallacies, things that you need to avoid, logical

**[01:03:23]** fallacies or problems in thinking.

**[01:03:25]** Low-hanging fruit. What's the obvious explanation?

**[01:03:27]** The immediate thing that comes to your mind?

**[01:03:29]** Just go with that. This is not how

**[01:03:31]** Bible study is done, because there

**[01:03:33]** is a lot of nuance. You're dealing with

**[01:03:35]** revelation from an infinite being. That means

**[01:03:37]** there is infinite depth. So the first

**[01:03:39]** thing that you see can't always

**[01:03:41]** be the immediate thing that you go with.

**[01:03:43]** You have to train yourself to be patient

**[01:03:45]** and to prefer nuance, to

**[01:03:47]** go for what is not obvious, to see

**[01:03:49]** hmm, maybe there's a different angle here.

**[01:03:51]** Let me accumulate knowledge from different

**[01:03:53]** places and see what the narrow

**[01:03:55]** road here is. And we'll talk more about this as we go along.
